# Release Plan — `controls-fail-loud`

**Milestone:** `controls-fail-loud` (ms#334) · Stage-4 sub-task **#7683** = the approved plan (parts 1–3), the Stage-4 gate **Decision Recorded** comment, the scaffolding record, the **Collective Review** scope-lock record, the pacing record, the D-Version re-determination and the short scope lock for #6237 · **#7801–#7807** = the seven Stage-5 designs (#4318, #5287, #4917, #7199, #6865, #6237, #6871 in that order), each with the hub's evaluation · **#7878–#7884** = the seven independent Phase A6.5 adversarial reviews, each with the hub's consumption · **#7808** = the Stage-6 Engineering sub-task whose spoke (card #4318, order 1) authored this file.

**Version identity:** **versioned** — bump-class **`minor`**, provisional display **`v4.70`**. Recorded as a determination (not a click-gate) at the Stage-4 gate, where the rule computed `v4.69`; `egress-hook-batch` then claimed `v4.69` at its merge, and the hub's D-Version re-determination moved the display to `v4.70`. The Commit-0 re-verify below re-ran both halves against fresh host state. The concrete `vX.Y` binds only at the Stage-12 atomic claim (ADR-092), so the plan file and the branch stay slug-primary while in flight and the Header `**Version**` cell carries the unresolved stamp placeholder.

**Topology:** D-C **SINGLE** — one release branch (`release/controls-fail-loud`), one PR opened in draft immediately after this commit so the pull-request checks run on every later slice (hub action item AI-009), one merge, base `main`.

**Concurrency posture:** **P0 fully-serial**, in the order #4318 → #5287 → #4917 → #7199 → #6865 → #6237 → #6871. Force-push, including `--force-with-lease`, is prohibited on the shared branch.

**Release class:** `cross-cutting` (trigger (c): four in-bundle compositional edges). Stage 9 review **Deep**. See § Release Class declaration.

> **Provenance.** This file transcribes the Stage-4 Release Planning output posted on #7683 (parts 1–3) and the Stage-4 gate **Decision Recorded** comment on it — whose amendments bind this commit (AC-Binding: an amended criterion obliges its bound row in the same change) — reconciled to the seven approved Stage-5 designs, the seven adversarial reviews and every operator decision recorded since: the **Collective Review** scope lock, the pacing decision, the D-Version re-determination and #6237's short scope lock. **Where a later disposition superseded a Stage-4 value, the transcribed section carries the ratified value and § Deviation Log records the delta with its authority.** Card-level design detail (change specifications, arm tables, region maps) stays in each card's Stage-5 output; each card's Stage-6 spoke transcribes what its commits realize and records any further delta here. Every thread comment consumed was `OWNER`-authored (Comment-Ingestion Trust Boundary).

## Commit-0 Version Re-Verify Record

Run in full at Engineering Commit 0, both halves, per `release/references/how-to/hub-spoke-bridge.md` Procedure 0 § Canonical location.

### Version half (steps 1–3, pre-write)

| Step | Action | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | `git fetch --tags origin`, then `git fetch origin main` | both exit 0; `origin/main` = `35dbf418` (the `v4.69` Stage-13 corpus merge), 35 commits past the Stage-4 baseline pin `8e0ee084` |
| **2** | Recompute next-free for bump-class **`minor`** through the adapter itself: `bash release/tools/claim-version.sh --sha 35dbf41847df2c1deab792d2944e46ac6ddd26fd --bump minor --dry-run` (the adapter's own `anchor()` + `claimed_set()`; no tag pushed) | **`v4.70`** (rc 0) — equal to the recorded provisional display |
| **3** | HALT on collision: the planned version must be absent from every `claimed_set()` arm AND equal the recomputed next-free. The tag arm binds; published Releases and the RELEASE_LOG corroborate | **no collision; PROCEED** |

**Probe record for the step-3 zero** (per `core/disciplines/review-discipline-principles.md` § 8, elements PV-0..PV-7):

```
Probe:       git ls-remote --tags origin 'refs/tags/v4.6*' 'refs/tags/v4.7*'     (tag arm — binds)
             grep -c '| v4\.70 ' <git show origin/main:release/releases/RELEASE_LOG.md>  (ledger arm)
             the adapter's claimed_set(), via --dry-run above                     (all three arms, unioned)
Denominator: every v4.6x/v4.7x tag on origin (v4.60 .. v4.69 plus v4.67.1); the RELEASE_LOG
             at origin/main, read with git show (never the worktree copy)
Control - sensitivity: the SAME readers on the v4.69 slot — tag arm: refs/tags/v4.69 present;
             ledger arm: 1 row (v4.69 egress-hook-batch VERIFIED). Every arm resolves and returns
             non-zero there, so a zero on the v4.70 slot is a real negative
Control - specificity: NOT TRIGGERED — a slot-occupancy question over an exact version tuple has
             no near-miss class
Extraction:  the full filtered ls-remote output; the full ledger read from origin/main
Result:      0 occupants of the (4,70) slot on every arm; the adapter recomputes v4.70
Verdict:     CLEAN — v4.70 is free and equals the recomputed next-free; no HALT
```

**In-flight state at Commit 0:** three sibling releases carry `release/*` heads and open draft PRs, and all three recompute to the same slot — see § In-Flight Release Roster. None has claimed; the Stage-12 atomic claim arbitrates, so this release may claim `v4.71` or higher.

### Manifest half (step 3b, post-write / pre-commit)

`release/tools/claim-version.sh --verify-stamp controls-fail-loud` — run after this file was written and before it was committed. Required exit **0**. Result recorded in § Verification Evidence.

This plan carries **exactly one** double-brace `RELEASE_VERSION` placeholder — the Header `**Version**` cell — and every other mention names the placeholder instead of reproducing it. The claim tool resolves the token by global substitution across the whole file, so a literal prose citation would be rewritten at Stage 12 along with the record site.

## Scope

**Seven cards, one cause class (C09: an instrument failure or an absent input rendered as a verdict), two limbs.** Limb A — a check that did not measure reports clean — is #4318, #5287, #4917 and #7199. Limb B — an instrument's own failure is reported as a verdict about its subject — is #6237, #6865 and #6871. No dependency edge crosses the limbs.

| # | Issue | Problem | Size | Labels |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | #4318 | The close-completeness probe declares a verdict-driven red exit its body does not implement: in warn mode it exits 0 on an INCOMPLETE verdict, and SKIP exits 0 too | M | bug · cluster: automation · project:pipeline · project:platform-quality |
| 2 | #5287 | Automated checks silently omit part of the surface they are believed to cover; nothing requires a check to declare its population or report what it examined | L | improvement · cluster: automation · project:governance-hygiene |
| 3 | #4917 | The never-FAIL class: three sub-shapes of a control reporting success without measuring, each needing a different fix | L | bug · cluster: automation · project:pipeline · project:platform-quality |
| 4 | #7199 | `release/README.md` advertises `release/schemas/*`, one of three roots that have never existed; the same roots sit in a check's scan list, a hook and a workflow | S | improvement · type:bug |
| 5 | #6865 | The fragile-reference hook fail-closes on a valid Write above the argument-size limit and reports it as malformed JSON | S | bug |
| 6 | #6237 | Checkpoint B's host-API axis renders PROCEED on an unstarted window it cannot distinguish from a fresh one | L | bug · project:platform-quality |
| 7 | #6871 | Close-out resolves the merge SHA over GraphQL, which is exhausted exactly when close-out runs | M | bug |

**Size:** 36 raw / 47 effective (`cross-cutting` × 1.3) after the Collective Review's re-sizes (CR-B4), held by an extended G3-15 Override. The Stage-4 figure was 26 raw / 34 effective.

**Acceptance criteria.** The criteria's single home is each issue body: 27 checkbox criteria across the seven cards, plus the Stage-4 amendment blocks appended to each body at the Stage-4 gate. § Verification Plan binds each checkbox criterion by ordinal (AC rows) and carries every Stage-4 amended or added criterion as an OBL row, because the AC-binding oracle reads only the checkbox list (hub action item AI-003).

**Explicit non-scope.** #7466 keeps only its criterion 5 (convert the fused exit-space sites outside Check 48) and stays unmilestoned (D-Dup-7466); the release PR references it and does not resolve it. The Check 32 Surface-1 residual is recorded, not fixed (#4917's census names it, dormant). The drift engine's own exit contract belongs to #4714.

### Pacing decision (operator, 2026-09-25)

The account's weekly allowance read 77% after the scope lock (reset 2026-10-01 05:00Z, a Thursday), and the remaining pipeline was estimated at more weekly points than were left. The hub recommended finishing #6237's re-design and pausing Stage 6 until the reset. **The operator chose to continue without a pause and to make every phase resumable from a new hub session** (a divergence from the recommendation). Resumability is carried by the staged briefs, the pending-approvals surface, a resume pointer, a Stage-6 resume check that continues from work banked on the release branch, and write-early commits.

### D-Version re-determination (hub, recorded determination)

`v4.69` was claimed by `egress-hook-batch` (its tag sits on that release's merge commit `40cec0c7`). With no `v4.70` tag on the remote and no `v4.70` ledger row at `origin/main` (the `v4.69` rows serving as the control), the provisional display moved to **`v4.70`**, re-verified at this commit (§ Commit-0 Version Re-Verify Record). The version still binds only at the Stage-12 atomic claim; `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare`, `closeout-verification-rows-consistent` and `telemetry-is-computable` contend for the same slot.

## Implementation Sequence

One branch (`release/controls-fail-loud`), P0, commits per card in the order below. **Limb A runs first, then limb B.** Each remediation lands an arm **observed RED against the pre-fix code** before its fix — or, where RED-first is impossible, an armed-red-then-revert record with its predictions stated before the run — recorded in § Verification Evidence (CIAC-6).

| Order | Card | What lands (locked design on its Stage-5 sub-task) | RED-first arm |
|---|---|---|---|
| **0** | — | **Engineering Commit 0:** this plan file, with the Commit-0 re-verify (steps 1–3, then 3b). The draft release PR opens immediately after it (AI-009) | n/a |
| **1** | **#4318** (#7801) | **Commit 1:** `_cc_verdict_exit_code` as the probe's only exit path, with the handshake line; the contract table and the cause-to-class table; the Surface-1 instrument (repository precondition + batch published-set read); the network-leg NOT-EVALUATED state on both surfaces, including drift exit 3; the lifecycle arm through `flag_not_evaluated`; `close-completeness.yml` rewired to an integer `case` that honors 2/3 only on the handshake; the register row restated; the sentinel's comments; the bridge sentence; every other declaration surface; self-test arms (8)–(15). **Commit 2:** limb (d) class-gated, the DENOM text, the schema row, RC-5b | arms (12)–(15) RED at the pre-fix probe (5 literal exits; INCOMPLETE/warn and SKIP exit 0; a failed lookup reported as absent; a binary RC test); RC-5b RED (1 version-less CHANGELOG finding) |
| **2** | **#5287** (#7802) | Requirement (b)'s `population:` field and Requirement (c)'s population-shortfall mode (S2: REQUIRED for file-root populations); the hoisted `_population_resolve` / `_population_report` pair and self-test group DP; Check 25 and Check 31 as the worked reference; the harness's fourth invariant with P6 on a synthetic ledger (CR-A5) and the C5 summary line fixed; the `install-tests.yml` comments; `decision-emission.yml`'s group count de-literalized | the harness real-instance arm (Check 25's four zero-yield roots reported) and the runtime arm, RED before the fix |

**Cross-card anchors.** Every card after order 1 edits files an earlier card touched, so **every spec anchor is located by quoted anchor text, never by line number.** Cards that share a file: `core/deploy/deploy.sh` (#4318, #5287, #4917, #7199), `core/standards/gate-efficacy-standard.md` (#4318, #5287, #4917), `core/deploy/tests/test_gate_efficacy_declarations.py` (#5287, #4917, #7199), `release/references/how-to/hub-spoke-bridge.md` (#4318's one sentence, then #6237's Checkpoint B regions), `core/hooks/block-fragile-refs.sh` (#7199, then #6865), `.github/workflows/install-tests.yml` (#5287, #4917).

**A2 container determination, recorded rather than assumed.** Every card is multi-file or structure-changing, so the threshold predicate selects the **GitHub sub-issue container**. The release runs each card's decomposition through the per-card Stage-6 sub-task the hub already scaffolded (#7808–#7814), with the change units enumerated in § File Change Matrix and rendered as checklist rows in the PR body; creating further sub-issues would duplicate a container the hub owns.

- **New-executable companion obligation.** Two `add` rows are executable. `release/tools/host-api-axis-verdict.sh` carries its companion `script-execution-allowlist.txt` row (the four house invocation forms) and a `selftest-coverage-manifest.txt` row, and it is CI-executed by `release-tooling-smoke.yml`'s self-test discovery job. `release/tools/lib/host-refusal-class.sh` is a sourced library with no CLI; per the zero-row precedent of the four existing `release/tools/lib/` libraries it takes no allowlist row, and every exercise of it runs inside an allowlisted consumer's self-test — a design decision locked at #6237's short scope lock, recorded here so the omission is visible rather than silent.
- **ADR numbering.** The two ADR rows are declared in glob form because each number binds at authoring (`release/tools/renumber-adr.py --detect` first). The mainline anchor at this commit is ADR-206; `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare`'s open PR carries ADR-207 to ADR-210 (R19). The census ADR's module is recorded as `core/ADRs/` because the census lives in a core standard; the order-3 spoke re-points the row if the record lands under `release/ADRs/`, where the index must then be regenerated.
- **Skill-package rebuild.** Only #6237 touches a rostered skill tree (release-hub), and it rebuilds `packages/release-hub.skill` plus its content sidecar in the same PR. Every other card's paths resolve to no skill (checked per card with `build-skill-packages.sh --skills-for-paths`, paths on stdin).

### Agent-Editability Read

Transcribed from the Stage-4 derivation (controls read at `8e0ee084`) and extended to the rows added since.

- **Tier-0 floor** (`core/hooks/block-autonomy-ceiling.sh`, the two `always_block "BLOCK-AUTONOMY-001"` blocks): the projected tracked union is the basenames `CLAUDE.md`, `OPERATIONS.md`, `RELEASE_PROTOCOL.md` at any depth. **No row matches**; `release/README.md` and `operations/README.md` have the basename `README.md`, and `RELEASE_PROTOCOL.md` appears only as text inside them.

### Contention

- **Within the release:** `core/deploy/deploy.sh` MULTI-WAY (#4318 → #5287 → #4917 → #7199), in distinct regions: #4318 the Check 48 engine, the probe, the lifecycle Check 48 arm, the usage line, the dispatch comment and its self-test arms; #5287 a hoisted pair after `flag_not_evaluated`, Checks 25 and 31 and group DP; #4917 Check 47's verdict tail and group BD; #7199 Check 25's root array (E3, by design). The gate-efficacy standard MULTI-WAY (row `:271`; Requirements (b)/(c); the census section). The harness MULTI-WAY (#5287 → #4917 → #7199). `hub-spoke-bridge.md` BINARY (#4318's one sentence at order 1, anchored on "Scaffold-independent enforcement of this Step 4 table"; #6237's Checkpoint B regions at order 6). `block-fragile-refs.sh` BINARY (#7199 → #6865). `install-tests.yml` BINARY (#5287 comments → #4917's one `run:` block). Serial P0 makes each a rebase-free sequence, never a conflict.
- **Cross-PR at Commit 0:** 3 open PRs (`gh pr list --state open --limit 500` → 3). `closeout-verification-rows-consistent` (#7895) intersects on `hub-spoke-bridge.md`, `stage-13-close.md` and `automated-closeout.sh`; `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare` (#7839) on the gate-efficacy standard and `install-tests.yml`, and it claims ADR-207 to ADR-210; `telemetry-is-computable` (#7901) carries only its plan file today, and its plan edits `deploy.sh`'s Check 48 engine in regions disjoint from #4318's (the telemetry cutoff default and its self-test group). A pinned measurement, not a verdict — Stage 9 A6.6 renders it.
- **Dormant siblings:** `obligations-get-runners` (#6872's Check 48 instance co-discharges with #4318's drain), `completion-asserts-completeness`, `release-instruments-measure-what-happened`, `restated-facts-derive-from-their-source` and `authoring-conventions-enforced-or-retired` — each in regions disjoint from this release's, per the Stage-4 Parallelization Map.

---

## Delivery Strategy

| Aspect | Decision |
|---|---|
| **Implementation approach** | Sequential (dependency-ordered), orders 1–7; limb A then limb B |
| **Commit strategy** | Per card, an arm observed RED (or a declared armed-red record) and then the fix commit(s). #4318's exit change, consumer rewire and register row go in **one** commit; #7199's hook and workflow arm removal in one commit. Messages carry `release(controls-fail-loud):` and reference the source card; signed; never `--no-verify` |
| **Review approach** | A single PR for the whole release (`release/controls-fail-loud` → `main`), opened in **draft** immediately after this commit so the pull-request checks — the hook tests among them — run on every later slice (AI-009). The PR body is parser-clean: close-family verbs adjacent to an issue number appear only in the Issue References block |
| **Deployment mechanism** | Git merge, the Stage-12 atomic claim, `deploy.sh --deploy release-hub` (package included), and a hash-verified hook-tier refresh |
| **Stacked-base cleanup posture** | Option A (default); no stacked bases |

---

## Verification Plan

**Every method cell carries its command literally** — reproducible from the cell alone, with pipes escaped for the table (`\|`); read them unescaped. A `bash …` cell is dispatchable but not executed by `release/tools/verify-release-plan.sh`, whose runnable-verb set is closed to read-only queries by design; such a row reads `UNRUNNABLE` there, with the reason `tool-invocation-outside-executor-allowlist:bash`, and its guarantee lives in the suite's own CI-invoked run. **OBL rows** carry the Stage-4 amended and added criteria (the AC-binding oracle reads only each issue's checkbox list, so an amendment-block criterion is invisible to it; OBL is its sanctioned channel for obligations outside that list — reported DISPLACED and excluded from the criterion set). Each OBL row names the criterion it carries.

**Probe P-7199-AC1** (from #7199's Stage-5 design V-1; FR-2), run from the repository root:

```
/usr/bin/python3 - release/README.md <<'PY'
import re,sys,subprocess,fnmatch,os
tr=[f for f in subprocess.run(["git","ls-files"],capture_output=True,text=True,check=True).stdout.split("\n") if f]
ds={f.rsplit("/",i)[0] for f in tr for i in range(1,f.count("/")+1)}
un,co,ok=[],[],0
for n,l in enumerate(open(sys.argv[1],encoding="utf-8"),1):
    for a,b in re.findall(r"`([^`]+)`|\]\(([^)#\s]+)",l):
        t=(a or b).strip().split(" ")[0].rstrip(".,;:")
        if t.startswith("../"): t=os.path.normpath(os.path.join("release",t))
        if not re.match(r"(release|core|operations)/",t): continue
        hit=any(fnmatch.fnmatchcase(f,t) for f in tr) if any(c in t for c in "*?[") else (t.rstrip("/") in ds or t in tr)
        if hit: ok+=1
        else: (co if "(when added)" in l else un).append(f":{n} {t}")
print(f"resolved={ok} unresolved={len(un)} conditional={len(co)}")
for x in un: print("  UNRESOLVED",x)
for x in co: print("  CONDITIONAL",x)
sys.exit(1 if un else 0)
PY
```

- [ ] **CIAC-5 (#6237 × #6871 on host refusal classification):** one vocabulary — a real call refused on quota grounds is exhaustion: never a probe failure (never fail-open) and never a state verdict (never "not merged"). *Method:* #6871's `--self-test` arms: a **REST** quota-refusal stub reports unresolvable (refused on quota grounds) and never `FAIL … false-VERIFIED`; a GraphQL `RATE_LIMIT` stub with REST answering reports merged; control: a MERGED stub reports PASS. The convention paragraph #6871 adds cites § 4.3b's refusal-reason classifier rather than restating it: `grep -c 'quota-budget-protocol.md' release/references/pipeline/stage-13-close.md` → ≥1 (the pin: 0). *Graded at Stage 9 QC3.5 on the merged PR.*
- [ ] **CIAC-6 (all seven, the release's own standard):** every remediation commit on the branch is preceded by an arm **observed RED against the pre-fix code**, or, where RED-first is impossible, by an armed-red-then-revert record whose predictions were stated before the run. *Method:* Stage 7 reads the branch log per card and this plan's § Verification Evidence; one RED observation (or declared armed-red record) per card, 7 of 7. *Graded at Stage 9 QC3.5 on the merged PR.*

**Population enumerated** for cross-issue cohesion: shared exit contract, shared standard section, shared scan roots, shared hook ↔ workflow mirror, shared refusal vocabulary, and the release's own RED-first standard. Index freshness and package freshness are per-card checks, kept under Release-Level Verification.

## Quota Budget

**Verdict:** **PASS**, conditional on the UNSTATED conservative default (`quota-budget-protocol.md` Checkpoint A). Parallel-eligible spokes: Stage 5: **7** · Stage 7: **7** · Stage 8: **7**; Stage 6 is serial by D-Concurrency. Per-spoke cost estimate: the size-bucket ordinal band (L: moderate–high — #5287, #4917, #6237 after CR-B4; M: low–moderate — #4318, #6871; S: lowest — #7199, #6865). The usage-window envelope was UNSTATED at planning, so `W_max` is **2** (rolling lanes of 2). Observed since: two Stage-5 spokes stopped at the session limit, which lowered the basis to SERIALIZE (the width-1 override above); the weekly allowance, which Checkpoint B does not model, read 77% after the scope lock, and the operator chose resumable pacing over a pause. Checkpoint B re-validates at every launch on both axes, DEFER-dominant.

## Release Class declaration

**`cross-cutting`** — trigger (c) fires: four in-bundle compositional edges (E1 #4318 → #4917, E2 #5287 → #4917, E3 #5287 → #7199, E4 #6237 → #6871). `novel` (b) also fires on the D-class decisions; multi-trigger resolution selects `cross-cutting`.

- **Differentiation posture:** Engagement **Tight** · Stage 9 review **Deep** · Stage 5 activation **ALL** · outcome window **30-day**.
- **Size bound:** `effective_pts: raw 36 × 1.3 = 47 — over band vs 15-25`, held by the extended G3-15 Override (CR-B4).

## Release Outcome Statement

### Release Outcome Statement

**AFTER** — A control that cannot evaluate its subject says so: a check that did not measure, measured less than it declares, or lost its own instrument reports that state as its own outcome — never as a pass, and never as a verdict about the thing it checks.

**BEFORE** — Controls render their own blindness as a result: the close-completeness probe exits 0 on an INCOMPLETE verdict, checks report clean over a smaller population than they declare, the pre-launch quota gate reads PROCEED from a pool reading it cannot tell apart from exhaustion, and a transport or exec failure surfaces as an unmerged PR or a malformed input.

**Actor(s):** the platform's CI gates, deploy-time checks and hooks, and the release hub's pre-launch quota gate.

**Success Indicator:** for every control this release touches, the Stage-7 evidence ledger records one input the control cannot evaluate, the pre-fix run reporting it as clean or as a verdict about its subject, and the post-fix run reporting a distinct not-evaluated or instrument-failure state (CIAC-6).

## Rollback Strategy

| Issue | Rollback method | Complexity |
|---|---|---|
| #4318 | `git revert` of its commits; commit 1's revert restores the exit contract, the consumer and the register row together (R17); commit 2's revert restores the CHANGELOG enumeration and the schema row together | Low |
| #5287, #4917 | `git revert` in reverse order (#4917 then #5287), because #4917's census is authored in #5287's form; the census ADR reverts with #4917 | Low–Med |
| #7199 | Revert #6865 first, or together with this card: its two stale-pattern-file arms depend on `is_durable`. Then `git revert` this card's commits, plus a hash-verified hook-tier refresh (the hook, its pattern file and the fixture runner revert together). If #5287 stays, reverting #7199 re-surfaces the dead roots as not evaluated — correct behavior, not a regression — and re-adds their ledger entries | Low–Med |
| #6865 | `git revert` plus a hash-verified hook-tier refresh (the deployed copy must revert too) | Med |
| #6237 | `git revert` plus a release-hub package rebuild and redeploy; revert #6871 first, because it sources #6237's classifier library | Med |
| #6871 | `git revert` (the self-test pin reverts with it) | Low |

**Whole release:** a partial revert for an isolated card failure; revert the merge commit for systemic failure; a forward fix for a well-understood minor issue. **A claimed version tag is retained and recorded, never deleted** — version tags are host-protected. After the claim, `git revert -m 1` stops once on this plan's claim-stamp rename: keep the stamped plan and continue.

The engine counts the VERIFIED rows the allowlist removes and decides on `cc_targets == 0` and `cc_allowlisted > 0`: nothing examined, at least one removed, the shape of PA-009's predicate. So a VERIFIED row the allowlist names, followed by a mid-close row, is the same SKIP. The state cannot hide a finding: a removed row reaches no limb, and the `.version` aggregate reads only rows that did. **Unchanged, and pinned:** a cutoff that arms while no row at/after it is VERIFIED, with nothing removed by the allowlist, keeps `CLEAN 0`, a complete scan of an empty set; a scope the allowlist only partly removes keeps its verdict over the rows that remain. **A reading recorded, not decided:** the allowlist test runs after the VERIFIED test, as it did before, so an allowlist entry that names a mid-close row removes nothing and that scope stays `CLEAN 0`; arm (7c) pins the order. The candidates projection prints the merged release's line format after a first line carrying the reply's item count, which is how the binding tells a full page from a short one although the head-label check drops items. **Two outcomes are refused rather than returned as a set that may be incomplete:** a full page that carries a pull request for another head (the host did not apply the head filter, so later pages cannot be trusted, and walking them would read the repository's whole pull-request population — the hazard the design review's FM-1 named for a paginated `state=all` read), and a full thirtieth page (the walk is bounded so that it ends). **No refusal is lost:** the merged release's slug-shape refusal, and this release's empty-branch and URL-safe-set refusals, each set the class, write their reason to stderr and exit 2 before any host call; a read that did not answer, or a refused set, exits 1. It selects the open rows through `_chore_pr_open_candidate`, which applies the resolver's own precedence (the highest-numbered open pull request), and it now records the phase outcome at both of its exits, as the merged release's header field requires. The projection compared the whole label exactly, so under a slug that differs from that spelling by letter case alone the reply held this run's own pull request and the re-check dropped it: the resume resolver read `NONE` where the same reply reads `OPEN` or `MERGED`, and the mainline's binding, which compares no label, finds it. Nothing foreign was ever bound, and this host is not affected. (b) **The decision, (A).** Correct it in this release, at one site, RED first, with a fourth review pass on this card authorized beyond the loop protocol's three. Not taken: (B) defer with a tracked item; (C) drop the owner part of the re-check. (c) **Measured first.** One line of the tool's production region compares a value the host reports with the configured slug's owner: the candidates arm of `_host_project`. The slug's three shape tests read the configured value alone, and the request's `head=` filter sends it. The binding's full-page refusal makes no comparison of its own — it counts the lines the projection printed against the items the page carried — so it inherited the defect and needs no edit. (d) **RED first (`a8d1a647`).** One fixture, `cand_case`: the reply `cand_one` carries, with the label's owner in the other letter case (`X:<branch>` under the pinned slug `x/y`). Predicted in the commit message before the run and observed at that commit: exit 1, `self-test: FAIL (1 failures)`, the one line that limb's, reading an empty value. (e) **The correction (`76019b67`), one comparison.** A label is this head when it equals the wanted head, as before, or when it splits into the same branch and an owner that differs only in the case of ASCII letters. **The split is at the first colon.** git allows no colon anywhere in a ref name (`git check-ref-format --branch 'chore/a:b'` exits 128, while a dot, an upper-case letter and `@#+=,` are accepted) and a host login carries none, so a well-formed label has exactly one; a second would fall in the branch part, which then cannot equal a wanted branch the binding has already held to a colon-free set. **Only A to Z fold onto a to z:** a host login is ASCII, so that is the whole of the host's own equivalence, and a character outside ASCII that Unicode lower-casing maps onto an ASCII letter stays a different character. **Everything else the re-check does stays, measured:** a table of 37 constructed replies, run through the projection cut from the tool's text at both revisions, differs on 7 rows, each an owner in another letter case on the same branch (one of the seven is an item whose state the projection refuses, which is now validated like any candidate's); the other 30 read the same before and after — a different owner, a different branch, a branch in another letter case, labels that are null, absent, not a string or wrongly split, and the Kelvin sign for `k` in either direction among them — and the fact, terminal and files projections are unchanged. | **DEV-121** | **#4318 — PA-016 (A): a body-drift engine that cannot be run is NOT-EVALUATED under its own cause, never a clean row. This row amends, by one state, the locked scope of NOT-EVALUATED (D-6, and #7878 F-1's scoping to the Surface-1 limb and the drift engine's reported exits), and adds a second token beyond Canonicalization #5's closed set, after DEV-37's.** (a) **The finding (Stage 8, QF-01, Minor; a fitness finding, not a criterion gap).** Check 48's body-drift limb ran its engine only when the engine file was executable, and that guard had no `else` arm. With the engine's path missing, or the file there not executable, the limb printed nothing: the row stayed counted as present and clean, and the gate could verdict CLEAN and exit 0 over rows whose published body was never compared. The state is latent on the shipped tree, where the engine is tracked at mode 100755; a rename that leaves a default path behind, a mode change, or an install that drops the executable bit reaches it. (b) **The decision, (A).** Correct it in this release, RED first, with the declarations and the register row brought into line, and with a fourth Stage-7 pass and a second QA pass on this card authorized beyond the loop protocol's three. Not taken: (B) defer with a tracked item and correct the declarations now. (c) **RED first (`35381e55`), the self-test region only.** Arm (14n) points `CC_DRIFT` at a path that holds no file and at a file without its executable bit, after the published set has listed the Release. It requires `NOT-EVALUATED 1 1 body-drift-unrunnable` for both on the gate surface, and on the lifecycle surface, with one aggregate line naming the cause and no finding line, and it asserts its own fixture first. Arm (14m) gains one denominator row for the state. Predicted in the commit message before the run and observed at that commit: exit 1, `self-test: FAIL (4 failure(s))` — the (14m) row reading `1 0 0 1` and three (14n) lines reading `CLEAN 1` — and no other line. (d) **The correction (`3f087db0`), one `else` arm.** It emits the row's `NOT-EVALUATED` record under the cause. The orchestrator already partitions that record out of the findings, moves the row from present to withheld in the network-leg denominator, and fans withheld rows in to one aggregate line naming the cause, so nothing else in the engine changes, and the arm prints no stderr line of its own (PV-7c). (e) **Why NOT-EVALUATED and not SKIP, and how the cause was named.** One limb of the network-scoped rows went unmeasured and every other limb of every row still ran; SKIP is the whole gate withheld. The operator's record names the verdict; the name and the class are this pass's, decided as Canonicalization #5 decided the set's. The survey is the eight tokens on the cause table: lowercase kebab-case, the body-drift limb's three sharing one prefix and each naming what the engine reported (its exit 2, its exit 3, any other exit). This state is the engine never having run, so it takes a fourth name under that prefix, **`body-drift-unrunnable`**, which covers the missing path and the non-executable file alike. `body-drift-missing` already names the engine's own exit 3, so no name built on "missing" was used. (f) **Its class: `failed-other` alone.** The cause-to-class table maps a cause onto the § 4.3b refusal classes. No host call is made in this state, so it is never refused-quota or failed-transport: it is a repository or install defect, an input failure a pull request can repair, and so a candidate for the cause-conditional escalation the enforce flip decides. Until that flip it exits 3 under every sentinel, as every NOT-EVALUATED cause does. (g) **Unchanged, and held by the standing arms:** the verdict precedence, the exit map, and every other state's verdict and exit. An engine that runs is read exactly as before. (h) **The cascade, in the correction's commit (OBL-7).** Changed: in `deploy.sh`, the engine header, the header of `_cc_row_findings`, the header of `_cc_verdict_exit_code`, the contract table's NOT-EVALUATED row, the cause-to-class table (one row), the named-residual paragraph and the probe's remedy line; in `close-completeness.yml`, the header's falsification line and the withheld-verdict summary, text only; in the gate-efficacy standard, the register row's instrument list and a sentence naming arms 14n and 14m, with the version-history clause that describes the row (a Tier 1 [ADJUST] beyond the brief's list, as DEV-113's was). Read and left: the `_cc_net_cause` list on the instrument resolver, which is the Surface-1 instrument's own set; the class-gate comment, which names one cause as its example; the header of `_cc_compute_verdict`, the usage line, the dispatch comment, the lifecycle Check 48 arm, the sentinel and the bridge sentence, each of which states the contract by verdict and lists no cause; and the census row for `_cc_row_findings`, which classifies what that consumer does with the engine's exit members — a state in which the engine never ran has none. (i) **Live, at `3db0383c`, through the probe's own seams** (each prediction written first). The real ledger with the engine present: `INCOMPLETE — 15 94`, exit 2, the network leg 80 present / 0 absent / 0 NOT-EVALUATED of 80. The same with the engine path missing: `INCOMPLETE — 2 94`, exit 2, 0 / 0 / 80 of 80, one aggregate line naming the cause for 80 rows, the DEGRADED rider, 0 drift findings, and the 2 Velocity findings kept. Stage 8 read this state at the entry head as `INCOMPLETE — 2 94` with 80 present and no notice. Over a finding-free scope (row cutoff `v4.31`: 51 rows, 44 of them network-scoped): CLEAN, exit 0, with the engine present; NOT-EVALUATED 44 of 44, exit 3, with the path missing under the warn sentinel, and again with a non-executable file under an enforce sentinel file. Every handshake equals its exit | Operator, PA-016 (the decision record on #7822, 2026-10-09), on Stage-8 QF-01 — Tier 2 [SCOPE CHANGE]; D-6; #7878 F-1; Canonicalization #5; DEV-37 | **APPLIED** — RED `35381e55`, GREEN `3f087db0` |

## Change Description

*Authored at Stage 6 Phase C1 per RELEASE_PROTOCOL § Change Description Protocol, by #4318's Engineering spoke at Commit 0, and refreshed by each card's spoke as its commits land. Operator-facing.*

### Outcome

**A control that cannot evaluate its subject now says so, instead of reporting a pass or a verdict about the thing it checks.** Seven controls change. The close-completeness gate stops exiting 0 on an INCOMPLETE verdict: 0 becomes CLEAN's alone, a finding exits an advisory 2, and a check that could not reach GitHub reports NOT-EVALUATED on 3 rather than 75 false "no published Release" findings. Checks must declare the population they scan and report what they examined, and four never-existing scan roots stop being swallowed. A census names, in three populations — the release-body drift engine's exit consumers, the workflow steps that reduce a captured exit status to zero-versus-non-zero, and the test suites with their executors — every member that could read "not measured" as "passed". Limb B fixes three instruments whose own failure was reported as a verdict: a hook that called a large valid Write "malformed", a quota gate that read PROCEED from a pool reading it cannot tell from exhaustion, and a close-out that called a merged PR unmerged when GraphQL ran out.

### Issues resolved

| # | Outcome (one line) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| #4318 | the close-completeness probe exits 0 only on CLEAN; INCOMPLETE is advisory 2, a withheld verdict is 3, and a failed GitHub lookup is NOT-EVALUATED, never "absent" | LANDED (`1eb7a2ee`, `dc81c7e1`, `e6fb603b`, `eeaa22be`) and verified — every verification row the design names ran, the last six in a second session after a GraphQL quota stop (DEV-41, DEV-42); **Stage-7 remediation** (`65bd4832`, `d106af31`, `437e5ec8`, `bca83983`; DEV-94 to DEV-97): a row cutoff that matches no LOG row is a withheld SKIP, never `CLEAN 0` (PA-004); **follow-up** (`fa99234b`, `a39e7d05`, `51ff3c5b`; DEV-113, DEV-114): an allowlist that names every VERIFIED row at/after the cutoff is the same withheld SKIP (PA-010), and the sentences that read a green gate say what it means (F-10); **Stage-8 follow-up** (`35381e55`, `3f087db0`, `3db0383c`; DEV-121, DEV-122): a body-drift engine that cannot be run — its path missing, or its file not executable — is NOT-EVALUATED under its own cause, never a clean row (PA-016, RED first), and the flip procedure says that an allowlist at the default path reaches the local check only (F-13) |
| #5287 | a check declares the population it covers and reports what it examined; an empty or short scan is reported, not passed | LANDED (`cb61349c`, `f6e3a166`, `2698748b`, `bf212643`, `59c2a8a2`, `63731209`) and verified — Checks 25 and 31 are the worked reference; Check 25's four never-existing roots are reported NOT-EVALUATED at runtime and ledgered in the conformance suite until order 4; **Stage-7 remediation** (`008ac124`, `3bc8dc02`, `0604f58b`, `e6bdc9b3`; DEV-98 to DEV-100): the conformance suite also checks the order of a check's resolve calls, and the standard reads the state table for a diff-scoped gate (PA-005) |
| #4917 | in three populations — the release-body drift engine's exit consumers, the workflow steps that reduce a captured exit status to zero-versus-non-zero, and the test suites with their executors — every member that could collapse "not evaluated" into "passed" is censused and asserted by a test | LANDED (`9d6b6fd6`, `99d36318`, `a19ac348`, `9b65c62b`, `cf6d5d98`, `341c8c8a`, `ac250aae`; the Q3 (B) follow-up `7d5668d1`, `f7eb50db`) and verified — Check 47 stops printing a clean verdict over rows it did not compare, and reports an undefined engine exit as a fail-closed instrument failure rather than as drift; the install-regression precision probe can fail again; the census's three records are re-derived and graded on every run, and ADR-217 records the decision; **Stage-7 remediation** (`437e5ec8`, `dad86016`; DEV-96, DEV-101 to DEV-103): text only; **second sync with main** (`7bab5632`; DEV-118 (d)): the merged mainline brought one new workflow exit-status consumer, and the conformance suite failed on it until the census recorded it, as designed |

Every payload read moves by here-string, so a write above the argument budget gets the verdict a small one gets. An input the hook cannot read is `INPUT-NOT-EVALUATED`: fail-closed in enforce, a WARN in warn. A detector constant that cannot compile takes the primitive gate's mode-coupled path. In macOS CI the hook harness failed at the RED commit `6e2930fb` and succeeded at `0d1e1417` and later, read at the Stage-7 remediation |

All six GraphQL-bound pull-request reads now go through one REST reader and the shared classifier, and read_state fails closed at `--apply` unless the release PR is answered as merged into `main`. In macOS CI the self-test failed at `a8d1a647` and succeeded at `76019b67`, `3c3b0da4` and `95640391`, read at Stage-7 iteration 4 and again in the text pass; **text pass before Stage 9** (`58cfbe14`, `0b2a1c02`; DEV-122; test only): one fixture item and one limb of arm u14 pin the branch half of the comparison and both sides of its owner half, so each of the two mutants iteration 4 measured surviving now fails exactly one line (PRT-u stays at 19 arms) |

### Key decisions

- **#4318 D-1 — the family's four-member exit contract** through one factored function, over a verdict-driven red exit (which needs a second sentinel reader) and the status quo (three producers of 0).
- **CR-A1 (ii) — an instrument that could not evaluate never gates by default:** Check 48's NOT-EVALUATED exits 3 under every sentinel (ADR-134 D5); escalation by cause is decided at the enforce flip, for input failures only.
- **CR-A3 — the drift engine's exit 3 is NOT-EVALUATED**, consistent with #4917's Check 47 conversion. **PA-016 — so is a drift engine that cannot be run**, its path missing or its file not executable, under its own cause (DEV-121).
- **D-Drain-48 — version-less rows are not asked for a CHANGELOG section**, and the governing schema moves in the same commit.
- **CR-A4 — `.claude/rules` is repointed to `core/rules`**, the corpus its check's standard names; **CR-B0 and the short scope lock** — #6237 re-designed around a charged probe and a shared, transport-aware classifier that #6871 sources.
- **CR-A2 (S2) — the population rule binds file-root populations this release**; a list or glob population, members inside files and a rows population are named non-detections, so no gate is asked for a declaration its grammar cannot express. **CR-A5** — the conformance suite's ledger arm runs on a synthetic ledger, so the empty ledger order 4 reaches cannot silence it.
- **D-Size, CR-B4 — kept whole at 47 effective** under an extended Override: one cause class with two limbs.
- **D-4917-3 (A) — Check 47 counts the rows its drift engine did not evaluate and reports them NOT-EVALUATED**, never as matches. **Q3 (B), the operator's call:** an exit outside the engine's contract is an instrument failure — not evaluated, named under its code, never a drift finding — and it fails the check closed.
- **CR-B1 (A′) — the precision probe derives its member list from the runner it copies**, with a passing control, instead of a hand-written deletion list that had gone stale.
- **CR-BUNDLE-2 — the census is keyed on status provenance and on the values compared, asserted on every run, and decided in ADR-217**; an unrecorded zero-executor suite is reported, not failed, leaving the merge decision with the executor-coverage owner.
- **CR-BUNDLE-4 — one durable-corpus scope predicate**, `is_durable` in the fragile-reference pattern file, read by the hook and the CI gate instead of two hand-copied lists; each surface refuses to run if the file it sourced lacks it.
- **CR-A2 (a) — the release's own population rule binds the gate it edits:** the reference-durability gate declares its population, reports what it examined, and turns a failed diff into NOT-EVALUATED and a red job — an input failure, never the clean line.
- **CR-A1 (ii) for #6865 — P2:** an input the fragile-reference hook could not read is `INPUT-NOT-EVALUATED`, mode-coupled per ADR-078: fail-closed in enforce, a WARN in warn. The design's fail-closed-in-every-mode posture would have turned warn mode's exit for that input class from 0 into 2. **CR-B2 (a′):** a compile canary for the four grep EREs at the constants gate, so a broken constant takes the gate's mode-coupled path once, before any detector, instead of reading as "no findings".
- **#6237 — the host-API axis reads its own charged probe (ML-D-1′, D-4′, D-6′, D-8 (a), D-9; ADR-218):** a repository-free REST probe and a GraphQL probe charged by selecting `__typename`, each read in-band and anchored on the probe's own draw inside the window its response's `Date` bounds; GraphQL read twice and re-probed once, then the most conservative reading as a calibration event; per-pool grades (`core` `[SOURCE]`, GraphQL `[ASSUMPTION – CONFIRM]`); the backoff capped at 3600 s, its count held on the DEFER action item. **ML-6871-D-2 = B2:** one transport-aware refusal classifier, `release/tools/lib/host-refusal-class.sh`, which #6871 sources.
- **#6871 — the close-out's pull-request reads (D-1, D-3, D-4, D-5; CR-B6; ML-6871-D-2):** one REST reader, classified by § 4.3b's shared classifier, with merged read from the pull request's merged flag and the SHA taken only when it is true; *failed for another reason* is unresolvable, never not merged (D-3); the await-merge poll stops at once on a quota refusal (D-4); the chore-PR lookup is owner-qualified and re-checked on the response, the owner compared there without regard to letter case and the branch exactly (D-5, #7884 FM-1; PA-013, DEV-120); read_state fails closed at `--apply` unless the release PR is answered as merged into `main`, a `DEFERRED` outcome excepting only the answered not-merged branch (CR-B6, DEV-89); the convention is stated once, in `stage-13-close.md`, citing § 4.3b rather than restating it (D-1, DEV-90).

### Reversibility

**MODERATE — HIGH confidence.** Each card reverts with `git revert` of its own commits (#4318's contract, consumer and register row as one commit), and reverting the merge restores `main`; the release-hub package and the deployed hook tier are refreshed on revert. The claimed version tag is retained and recorded, never deleted.

### Downstream impact

- **`deploy.sh --check` gains two population records.** Check 25 reads `status=fetched declared=11 resolved=11 examined=481`: its three never-existing release roots are gone, and `core/rules` replaces `.claude/rules`, bringing the rules corpus its standard declares into the scan (22 more files; 2 more DC2 signals, no DC1 finding on the tracked patterns — the operator's needle-file read is still owed before merge). The conformance suite's residual ledger is empty, and it fails if a future entry outlives its root.
- **The reference-durability gate prints what it examined** on every run — changed files, those in the durable corpus, those exempted and those examined — and its clean line states the examined count. A failed diff now turns this required context red with NOT-EVALUATED instead of reporting clean.
- **The fragile-reference hook and its pattern file must deploy together.** The pattern file now carries the scope predicate, and the hook refuses a pattern file without it: it stands down in warn and fails closed in enforce. Until the Stage-12 refresh, Check 79 reports both deployed copies as advisory drift, and since #6865 a third, the fixture runner's (DEV-77).
- **The fragile-reference hook adjudicates a write of any size.** A Write or Edit above the argument budget is no longer refused as malformed JSON. An input the hook cannot read, because no temp file can be written, is reported as `INPUT-NOT-EVALUATED`. In enforce that blocks every Write and Edit, in scope or not, because validation precedes the scope gate; in warn it warns. The `INPUT-INVALID` line prints jq's exit code. A detector constant that cannot compile stands the hook down in warn and fails it closed in enforce.
- **The close-completeness gate goes advisory-visible:** 15 real findings (13 §5.1 body drifts, 2 Velocity fields) become a standing warning on every in-roster PR until a §5.6 re-emit sweep drains them; the sentinel cannot flip to `enforce` before that drain.
- **The flip to enforce is now a three-step clause plus a cause-conditional escalation decision**, recorded in the register row and the sentinel file. Both now say that an allowlist at the engine's default path is git-ignored and reaches the local check only (DEV-122). The cause table that decision reads gains `body-drift-unrunnable`, an input failure with no host call, and so a candidate for escalation (DEV-121).
- **Stage 12** refreshes the hook tier by hash, deploys the rebuilt release-hub package and refreshes the script-execution allowlist through the update path (manifest row 4). The version claim no longer contends for `v4.70`: `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare` bound that slot at its merge, and the three releases merged since bound the next three. So the provisional display that this plan's front matter and Header still carry is no longer claimable; the atomic claim recomputes upward (R9), and the display is the hub's to re-determine (DEV-118 (k)). Of the three siblings the roster measured, only `telemetry-is-computable` is still in flight.
- **Checkpoint B's host-API axis is an executable.** The hub runs `release/tools/host-api-axis-verdict.sh` once per routing turn — three metered requests, at most five — reads its `HOST-API` record only on exit 0, and renders its `RENDER` line verbatim. A reading without the probe's own draw is `UNANCHORED` and DEFERs. A quota refusal DEFERs with the recovery its evidence supports, and the hub carries `refusals_in_a_row` on the open DEFER action item and passes it back as `--prior-refusals`. It reaches the running hub at the Stage-12 deploy, once manifest row 4 lets the hub run it; this release's own launches use the old axis (R8).
- **ADR-218 supersedes ADR-156 in part** (the instrument and its evidence grade; the unstarted-window residual). It was authored as 208. It moved to 212 at the first sync with main (DEV-117), because `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare` merged first and bound 207 to 210, and to 218 at the second sync (DEV-118), because three more releases merged first and bound 211 to 216. The record's Status section carries one numbering-provenance note per move, and ADR-156's `superseded_by:` and Related ADRs link name ADR-218. Only the mainline binds a number: a release that merges before this one and binds 218 moves this record again, at this release's Stage-12 claim.
- **The release-hub `version:` field reads `v4.70`**, the plan's provisional display. A sibling release, `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare`, claimed that slot first, so the field names that release's version until a sanctioned restamp (R9, DEV-83).
- **`deploy.sh --check` Check 47 gains a population record** (`status=fetched`, `degraded` or `not-run`): a run that cannot resolve `origin/main` now reads not evaluated rather than 94 matches. An exit the drift engine's contract does not define is an instrument failure: the row is not evaluated and never read as drift, and the check fails closed with one issue and no OK line.
- **A new workflow step that compares a captured exit status only against 0, or a new drift-engine caller, fails the conformance suite** until the census records it with a direction, a classification and a reason.
- **The census record is ADR-217.** It was authored as 207. It moved to 211 at the first sync with main (DEV-117), because `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare` merged first and bound 207 to 210, and to 217 at the second sync (DEV-118), because three more releases merged first and bound 211 to 216. The record's Status section carries one numbering-provenance note per move. Only the mainline binds a number: a release that merges before this one and binds 217 moves this record again, at this release's Stage-12 claim.
- **The close-out reads every pull request over REST, through one classified reader.** A GraphQL pool drained by concurrent releases no longer stops a close whose merge is real. A read that does not answer is named by its class — refused on quota grounds, failed in transport, or failed for another reason — and is never reported as not merged, false-VERIFIED or absent.
- **`read_state` now stops an `--apply` run before any write** unless the release PR is answered as merged into `main`; `--dry-run` records a non-blocking WARN that predicts the stop. A mistyped `--pr`, or a merge fact the host did not answer, now halts at phase 3 with exit 3 — before VERIFIED is stamped and the milestone closes. A close with `--outcome DEFERRED` records its unmerged release PR instead.
- **The chore-PR lookup fails on a read that does not answer** rather than creating a PR, and binds only a PR whose head is this repository's chore branch. It is one binding, shared with the resume resolver that `closeout-verification-rows-consistent` added: it reads every state and every page through the classified reader, and it refuses, rather than returns, a candidate set it cannot establish as complete (DEV-119). It finds this run's own PR whatever letter case the configured handle is typed in, because the host matches and reports the owner in its own spelling (DEV-120). The await-merge poll stops at once on a quota refusal or a non-transient failure, and still re-polls a transport failure or a server error. Verification row 4 gains `UNVERIFIED (…)` for a merge fact it could not read, and its Method cell reads `grep + REST pulls/<n>`. The release-diff fallback treats a full thirtieth page of files as a truncation, never as the complete diff.
- **The close-out sources `release/tools/lib/host-refusal-class.sh`**, the classifier #6237 authored, so reverting #6237 needs #6871 reverted first (§ Rollback Strategy).
- **Cross-release overlap in the close-out tool.** The overlap with `closeout-verification-rows-consistent` (#7436) is settled: that release merged first, and the second sync reconciled the two definitions of `_host_chore_pr_candidates` to one, under the operator's decision PA-012, and re-based its `_chore_pr_terminal_state` paragraph and its resume group's stub on this release's REST reader (DEV-118, DEV-119). **Still open, for Stage 9:** `telemetry-is-computable` (#6252) edits `phase_read_state`'s cycle-time block, directly below this card's merge-fact region, and shares its `mark_phase "read_state"` line. Whichever of the two merges second re-baselines; the regions are named by anchor text in the order-7 Stage-6 output.
- **Named residuals routed, not fixed:** the drift engine's failed body read surfacing as drift (#4714); the family's advisory-code handshake (#7889); #7466 criterion 5; the other hooks' pinned-`printf` idiom and the hook tree's `HOOK-ERROR` token, which lacks PV-7a's clause (both #7835); the gh CLI's fused exit space at four sites, Check 32's dormant cutoff among them (#6620); the zero-executor `test_version_freeness.sh` (#6114); the exit-2 arm's doubled `head` path in Check 47 (#7034); the write-scoped half of presentation (c), request rate and mutation weight (#7847); a wait loop's fixed-cadence polling of the host (#7427); the budget probe and refusal binding as named gaps of the `repo_host` interface (#10); the close-out's remaining GraphQL-bound writes and issue reads, and the Stage-12 merge verification with its test-merge trap (#7876); the error-body capture under `--jq` on the latest-Release read (#7868's class) and on the milestone-state read (routed to intake).

### Cross-references

- Release plan: this file — § Decision Record, § Verification Plan, § Deviation Log.
- Milestone: `controls-fail-loud`.
- User-facing release notes: `release/releases/notes/vX.Y_RELEASE_NOTES.md`, authored at Stage 13 Close per `release/references/standards/release-notes-standard.md`.

## Baseline pin

`origin/main` @ **`4babbba9`** (`4babbba94f3ddf99a658d39fc0dbb7af0f664066`) — the release branch's synchronized base since the second Stage-6 sync with main, which merged exactly this commit into the branch (`c6a8da57`, DEV-118). Read by the Stage-9 mid-pipeline divergence re-check.

**The first synchronized base, from the first sync until the second:** `ef5a952d` (`ef5a952d77f0e28eb6bac862ccdd61a433d34935`), merged as `1372aa01` (DEV-117). The mainline then advanced to `4babbba9` with three more releases and their close-outs — `work-nature-and-axis-model`, `closeout-verification-rows-consistent` and `install-resolves-identically` — and with one dependency update and one security-policy change. Together they change ten files this branch also changes, four of them in conflict, and claim ADR numbers 211 to 216; the second sync merged them.

**The original pin, Stage 4 until the first sync:** `8e0ee084` (`8e0ee08450a5e1d64f352279a3ab0f4a6ce46f6d`), the v4.68 Stage-13 corpus merge, measured at Stage-4 Phase A0 and carried by every Stage-5 design. The mainline then advanced to `ef5a952d` with the `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare` release and its close-out, which touch three rows of this matrix — the gate-efficacy standard, `install-tests.yml` and the release ADR index — and claim ADR numbers 207 to 210; the first sync merged them.

**The release branch's original base:** `35dbf418` (`35dbf41847df2c1deab792d2944e46ac6ddd26fd`), `origin/main` at Engineering Commit 0 — 35 commits and 18 files past the pin (the `v4.69` merge and its close-out). **0 of those 18 files is an edited row of this matrix**; the one matrix-adjacent path, `core/rules/bypass-mode-readiness.md`, is a CONDITIONAL row that resolved false (DEV-15). Probe: `git diff --name-status 8e0ee084 35dbf418` → 18 files; control: the same diff restricted to #4318's six matrix paths → 0, against 18 for the whole tree.

## Issue References

The seven content members of this milestone are resolved by the release PR's Issue References block; #7466 is referenced only.

- **#4318** — the close-completeness probe declares a verdict-driven red exit its body does not implement. Four checkbox criteria plus four added at the Stage-4 gate.
- **#5287** — automated checks silently omit part of the surface they are believed to cover. Five criteria.
- **#4917** — the never-FAIL class: three sub-shapes of a control reporting success without measuring. Four criteria.
- **#7199** — `release/README.md` advertises roots that have never existed. Two criteria.
- **#6865** — the fragile-reference hook fail-closes on a large valid Write and reports it as malformed JSON. Four criteria.
- **#6237** — Checkpoint B's host-API axis renders PROCEED on an indistinguishable window. Four criteria.
- **#6871** — close-out resolves the merge SHA over GraphQL. Four criteria.
- **#7466** — the duplicate whose criteria were allocated to #4318 and #4917; it keeps criterion 5.
- **#4714** — owns the drift engine's exit contract (its failed body read, routed from #4318).
- **#7889** — the advisory-code handshake defect across the exit-code family (routed from #4318's review).
- **#6620** — owns Check 32's dormant cutoff (cited by #4917's census).
- **#6872** — the `obligations-get-runners` card whose Check 48 instance co-discharges with #4318's drain.
- **#7835** — the class card for the pinned-external-`printf` idiom in the other hooks, which reuses #6865's remedy form; it also carries the `HOOK-ERROR` clause residual.
- **#7683 · #7801–#7807 · #7878–#7884 · #7808–#7814** — the Stage-4, Stage-5, Phase A6.5 and Stage-6 hub sub-tasks carrying the plan source, the designs, the reviews and the decision records.
